# Skills · F · part 14

986 entries, fitts-law to fixed-asset-accounting. [Index](README.md) · [Everything](../README.md)

- **fitts-law**: Apply Fitts's Law — target acquisition time depends on size and distance. Use when sizing and positioning controls, especially for touch. For how many controls to show at once, use `hicks-law`. ([Owl-Listener/designer-skills](https://github.com/Owl-Listener/designer-skills/tree/HEAD/interaction-design/skills/fitts-law) · 1.3k installs)
- **fitts-law-pointer-acceleration**: Use this skill when the question involves cursor speed, pointer acceleration curves, or interaction with input devices that vary in precision (mouse, trackpad, stylus, touchscreen, eye-tracking). Trigger when designing for varied input devices, building drag interactions, designing scrubbers / sliders / handles, or analyzing why a UI feels "fiddly." Sub-aspect of `fitts-law`; read that first if you haven't already. ([Deibler/universal-design-principles](https://github.com/Deibler/universal-design-principles/tree/HEAD/plugins/interaction-and-control-principles/skills/fitts-law-pointer-acceleration) · ★8)
- **fitts-law-screen-edges**: Use this skill when designing for the edges and corners of the screen — where the cursor cannot overshoot, making them effectively infinite-sized targets along one axis. Trigger when designing global navigation, system menus, sticky headers, fixed sidebars, full-bleed CTAs, or anywhere you can put the most-frequent or hardest-to-find actions. Trigger when reviewing an app that has wasteful margins on every side and asks the user to aim for tiny targets in the middle. Sub-aspect of `fitts-law`; read that first if you haven't already. ([Deibler/universal-design-principles](https://github.com/Deibler/universal-design-principles/tree/HEAD/plugins/interaction-and-control-principles/skills/fitts-law-screen-edges) · ★8)
- **fitts-law-touch-targets**: Use this skill when designing for touch — phones, tablets, kiosks, in-car displays, smart-TV remotes, anything where the input is a finger or thumb rather than a precise pointer. Trigger when sizing buttons for mobile, debugging "users keep mistapping," designing a mobile bottom-nav, picking icon sizes for a phone toolbar, or reviewing whether a desktop UI is touch-friendly. Sub-aspect of `fitts-law`; read that first if you haven't already. ([Deibler/universal-design-principles](https://github.com/Deibler/universal-design-principles/tree/HEAD/plugins/interaction-and-control-principles/skills/fitts-law-touch-targets) · ★8)
